Then NtQueryInformationThread(ThreadQuerySetWin32StartAddress) on that thread and REQUIRE it == liveModuleBase + 0x5CADA90 (AddressOfEntryPoint, read from the PE header). Call it TID_E2. [Justification: Launch.cpp:88-92 + :178-183, LaunchWindows.cpp:179-260, Core.Build.cs:298-301.]\\n  0c. REQUIRE TID_E1 == TID_E2. This is the first independent agreement, and it is obtained entirely before the callback exists.\\n  0d. For that TID: NtQueryInformationThread(ThreadBasicInformation) -> TebBaseAddress; ReadProcessMemory TEB+0x08 (StackBase), TEB+0x10 (StackLimit), TEB+0x48 (ClientId.UniqueThread). REQUIRE ClientId.UniqueThread == TID_E1. Persist {TID_GT, TebBase, StackLimit, StackBase}.\\n\\nPHASE 1, the record block (owned by the injector, NOT by the MinGW DLL):\\n  VirtualAllocEx a single RW page in the target. Layout, all POD, all naturally aligned:\\n    +0x00 u32 magic          (0x47543031 'GT01', written at arm time)\\n    +0x04 u32 hitCount       (0, bumped by InterlockedIncrement as the FIRST act of the callback)\\n    +0x08 u32 tid            (sentinel 0xFFFFFFFF)   <- I1\\n    +0x0C u32 injectorTid    (written at arm time from the injector thread) <- negative control N4\\n    +0x10 u64 tebPtr         (0)  <- I2, from gs:[0x30]\\n    +0x18 u64 rsp            (0)  <- I3\\n    +0x20 u64 retAddr0       (0)  <- I4, __builtin_return_address(0)\\n    +0x28 u64 retAddr1       (0)  <- I4', __builtin_return_address(1)\\n    +0x30 u64 qpc            (0)\\n    +0x38 u64 ctxPtr         (0)  <- whatever pointer the mechanism handed the callback\\n    +0x40 u32 selfTestTid / +0x44 u64 selfTestTeb / ... <- N1 slot, a duplicate of the same fields\\n  The callback body is ~15 instructions: InterlockedIncrement, then plain stores. No FString, TArray, TFunction, TDelegate, TSharedPtr, no allocation, no engine call. Nothing crosses the MinGW<->MSVC C++ ABI — the constraint the owner set is satisfied by construction.\\n  The record block being injector-owned matters twice over: (i) the read-only instrument, not the write-capable one, renders the verdict; (ii) it survives FreeLibrary of the probe DLL.\\n\\nPHASE 2, the three negative controls — run BEFORE trusting any positive result:\\n  N1 (discrimination control): call the identical recorder function directly, synchronously, on the injected CreateRemoteThread thread, into the selfTest slot. It MUST produce a different TID, a different TEB pointer, an RSP outside [StackLimit, StackBase) of TID_GT, and a return address that is NOT inside MISERY .text RVA [0x1000, 0x5DCAA9C). If the recorder cannot tell our own thread apart from the GameThread, every later reading is worthless. This is the owner's explicit requirement that the injected thread's TID be shown to differ.\\n  N2 (no-spurious-fill control): arm nothing, wait the same wall-clock timeout the real run will use, ReadProcessMemory the block. REQUIRE hitCount == 0 and tid == 0xFFFFFFFF. Proves the record is only written by the dispatch.\\n  N3 (falsifiability): the mechanism must be able to REPORT FAILURE, not just succeed. Bound the wait. If hitCount is still 0 at timeout, the verdict is FAIL and the hook is removed regardless. A mechanism that cannot fail cannot prove anything.\\n\\nPHASE 3, the real one-shot, and the agreement rule:\\n  Arm, dispatch, wait, disarm. Then read the block ONCE and apply ALL of:\\n    A1. hitCount == 1 (exactly once). Re-read after a further 5 s: still 1.\\n    A2. tid == TID_GT (== TID_E1 == TID_E2).\\n    A3. tebPtr == TebBase from 0d.                       [independent of A2 — no thread id involved]\\n    A4. StackLimit <= rsp < StackBase.                   [independent of A2 and A3]\\n    A5. (retAddr0 - liveModuleBase) in [0x1000, 0x5DCAA9C), i.e. inside MISERY .text — or retAddr1 if the immediate caller is our own thunk page. Then resolve it offline against the 411,385 .pdata RUNTIME_FUNCTION records (.pdata RVA 0x07CCF000, size 0x004B53AC) to name the enclosing function's start RVA and record it.\\n    A6. Re-run 0a and 0b AFTER the callback. TID_E1 and TID_E2 must be unchanged. (Guards against the GameThread having been re-created, or the process having restarted, between arm and fire.)\\n  A2 through A5 are four observables acquired by four different mechanisms — a kernel32 call, a segment-relative TEB load, a register read, and a stack return slot. They are not restatements of one another. If A2 holds but A4 fails, we were handed a fabricated TID; if A2 and A4 hold but A5 fails, we called ourselves rather than being called by the engine. Only the full conjunction supports the claim 'the callback executed on the Unreal GameThread'.\\n  ONLY if A1-A6 all pass does phase 2 of GT-01 (the single KismetSystemLibrary::LoadAsset_Blocking on /Game/ModKit/MK_Canary.MK_Canary, located strictly through reflection) become authorized.\\n\\nWHY THIS AND NOT GGameThreadId: it is a plain uint32 global (CoreGlobals.cpp:428) — trivially readable IF we knew where it was — but it is zero-initialised, so it lives somewhere in the ~3.5 MB BSS tail of .data (RVA [0x07941600, 0x07CCE62C)) with no string, export, relocation or RTTI to pin it, and any dynamic scan for it is circular because the search key IS the TID we are trying to prove. Worse, in this Shipping build GGameThreadId is not even the engine's own decision variable: IsInGameThread() reduces to the thread_local ActiveTaskTag check (ThreadingBase.cpp:177-198), and the exe's total absence of that path's DO_CHECK strings confirms it. E1 needs no address in the target image at all; E2 needs only a PE header field. That is why the name-and-initial-thread pair is the rigorous route and the global is not.", "disqualified": ["GGameThreadId read as an ORACLE — the global is a plain uint32 (CoreGlobals.cpp:428, CoreGlobals.h:550), but its address is NOT rigorously identifiable in this stripped binary (zero-init => BSS tail of .data, RVA [0x07941600, 0x07CCE62C), ~3.5 MB, no string/export/reloc/RTTI anchor). It also self-removes, so there is no hook to deactivate. HOWEVER two independent hard blockers disqualify the whole family for the injected-DLL scenario: (1) ADDRESS IDENTIFIABILITY — none of AddTicker/GetCoreTicker/AsyncTask/CreateAndDispatchWhenReady is exported, none has a PDB, none has a surviving anchor string (the Ticker checkf 'Invalid state %u' is 0/0 in the exe), FTSTicker is non-polymorphic so it has no vtable/RTTI (S-10's 628 descriptors contain zero Ticker/Task/Delegate entries), and none is a UObject/UFunction so reflection — the project's proven NAME->ADDRESS oracle — cannot reach them. There is no rigorous, project-permitted way to obtain a callable address; only signature guessing, which is forbidden. (2) ABI — every entry point in this family takes a TFunction/TUniqueFunction/FTickerDelegate by value or reference, non-POD objects whose 32-byte inline TFunction_OwnedObject vtable and storage contract (Function.h:120-296, CoreDefines.h:61) are emitted by MSVC and cannot be constructed from MinGW g++, exactly the ABI hazard the task defines as disqualifying. int32 ObjectSerialNumber;` | `WeakObjectPtr.h:318-319` | 0 / 4 | 4 / 4 | 4 |\n| `FSoftObjectPath` | `FTopLevelAssetPath AssetPath;` | `SoftObjectPath.h:341` | 0 | 16 | 4 |\n| `FSoftObjectPath` | `FString SubPathString;` | `SoftObjectPath.h:344` | 16 | 16 | 8 |\n| `FTopLevelAssetPath` | `FName PackageName;` | `TopLevelAssetPath.h:176` | 0 | 8 | 4 |\n| `FTopLevelAssetPath` | `FName AssetName;` | `TopLevelAssetPath.h:178` | 8 | 8 | 4 |\n| `FName` (Shipping) | `FNameEntryId ComparisonIndex; uint32 Number;` | `NameTypes.h:1109, 1112` | 0 / 4 | 4 / 4 | 4 |\n| `FString` | `TArray<TCHAR, TSizedDefaultAllocator<32>> Data;` | `UnrealString.h.inl:56-57` | — | 16 | 8 |\n| `TArray` | `ElementAllocatorType AllocatorInstance; SizeType ArrayNum; SizeType ArrayMax;` | `Array.h:3230-3232` | 0 / 8 / 12 | 8 / 4 / 4 | 8 |\n\n`FName` is 8 bytes in this build because `WITH_CASE_PRESERVING_NAME` defaults to `WITH_EDITORONLY_DATA` (`NameTypes.h:30-32`, = 0 in Shipping) and `UE_FNAME_OUTLINE_NUMBER` defaults to 0 (`NameTypes.h:36-38`), so both the `#if UE_FNAME_OUTLINE_NUMBER` and `#if WITH_CASE_PRESERVING_NAME` members at `NameTypes.h:1110-1117` compile out. **Caveat (INFERRED):** those are `#ifndef` guards; a target could override them. Cheap live cross-check below.\n\n`sizeof(TSoftObjectPtr<UObject>) = 40 (0x28)`, `alignof = 8`. No virtuals anywhere in that chain (checked all four headers) → no vptr.\n\n### Parms buffer for `LoadAsset_Blocking` (INFERRED from the above + UHT declaration-order linking)\n\n```\nParms (48 bytes, zero-filled before the call)\n+0x00  Asset : FSoftObjectProperty, ElementSize 0x28\n       +0x00  int32  WeakPtr.ObjectIndex            = 0\n       +0x04  int32  WeakPtr.ObjectSerialNumber     = 0\n       +0x08  uint32 AssetPath.PackageName.ComparisonIndex\n       +0x0C  uint32 AssetPath.PackageName.Number\n       +0x10  uint32 AssetPath.AssetName.ComparisonIndex\n       +0x14  uint32 AssetPath.AssetName.Number\n       +0x18  TCHAR* SubPathString.Data              = nullptr   <-- NON-POD FString\n       +0x20  int32  SubPathString.ArrayNum          = 0\n       +0x24  int32  SubPathString.ArrayMax          = 0\n+0x28  ReturnValue : FObjectProperty, UObject*, 8 bytes\n+0x30  == ParmsSize\n```\nPredicted `UFunction::ParmsSize = 0x30 (48)`, `UFunction::ReturnValueOffset = 0x28 (40)`.\n`ElementSize` of the soft-object parm comes from `TProperty::CPPSize = sizeof(TCppType)` (`UnrealType.h:1254`) with `FSoftObjectProperty : TFObjectPropertyBase<FSoftObjectPtr>` (`UnrealType.h:3100`).\n\n### Reading `ParmsSize` / `ReturnValueOffset` at runtime (how this project already does it)\n\n`research/instruments/eri/eri.py:4797-4812`, all relative to the `UFunction` object's own address:\n\n```\nUSTRUCT_TOTAL_SIZE_SHIPPING            = 0xB0\nUFUNCTION_FUNCTION_FLAGS_OFFSET        = 0xB0   # EFunctionFlags uint32   (Class.h:1797)\nUFUNCTION_NUM_PARMS_OFFSET             = 0xB4   # uint8  NumParms         (Class.h:1802)\nUFUNCTION_PARMS_SIZE_OFFSET            = 0xB6   # uint16 ParmsSize        (Class.h:1804; +0xB5 is padding)\nUFUNCTION_RETURN_VALUE_OFFSET_OFFSET   = 0xB8   # uint16 ReturnValueOffset(Class.h:1806)\n```\n`0xB0` is live-confirmed by ERI's own `num_parms_cross_check` (I-05 records carry `num_parms_matches_accepted_count`). **Do not hardcode 0x30/0x28 — read them and assert they equal 0x30/0x28.** A mismatch means one of the `#ifndef` assumptions above is wrong and the escalation must stop. ESC-01 already worked this way (`research/decisions.md`, ESC-01 row `outcome`: `parms_before=0x00`, `parms_after=0x00`, `ReturnValueOffset=0`).\n\nTwo more free cross-checks, both read-only:\n- `/Script/CoreUObject.SoftObjectPath` exists as a UScriptStruct in this game (`script-objects.tsv:5389 … package_member_unclassified /Script/CoreUObject/SoftObjectPath`). Reading its `UStruct::PropertiesSize` should give **32** and `MinAlignment` **8**. Source order is `SuperStruct, Children, ChildProperties, PropertiesSize, MinAlignment, Script, PropertyLink` (`Class.h:394-411`); ERI already has verified anchors `USTRUCT_CHILDREN_OFFSET = 0x48` (eri.py:4758) and `USTRUCT_CHILD_PROPERTIES_OFFSET = 0x50` (eri.py:3537), which puts `PropertiesSize` at **+0x58** and `MinAlignment` at **+0x5C** — INFERRED, not yet verified in this project.\n- Walk `LoadAsset_Blocking`'s `ChildProperties` with I-06 and confirm exactly two entries: `Asset` (FSoftObjectProperty, offset 0, size 40) and `ReturnValue` (FObjectProperty, offset 40, size 8, `CPF_ReturnParm`).\n\n### The non-POD problem, and why it is solvable\n\nThe **only** non-POD member in the whole 48-byte buffer is `SubPathString` (`FString`) at `Parms+0x18`.\n\n**An empty `FString` IS safely representable as zeroed memory.** `FString` is exactly a `TArray<TCHAR, TSizedDefaultAllocator<32>>` (`UnrealString.h.inl:51-57`), i.e. `{Data=nullptr, ArrayNum=0, ArrayMax=0}`. The heap allocator's destructor is `FORCEINLINE ~ForAnyElementType() { if(Data) { … FMemory::Free(Data); } }` (`ContainerAllocationPolicies.h:471-481`) — null-guarded. `FString::Len()` returns `Data.Num() ? Data.Num()-1 : 0` (`UnrealString.h.inl:1082-1085`) → 0. So a zeroed 16 bytes is a valid, destructible, empty `FString`. **Zero-filling the Parms buffer is therefore not just hygiene, it is what makes the FString legal.** Likewise `FWeakObjectPtr{0,0}`: `Internal_GetObjectItem()` returns `nullptr` immediately when `ObjectSerialNumber == 0` (`WeakObjectPtr.h:265-278`), and the only thing distinguishing the two `#if UE_WEAKOBJECTPTR_ZEROINIT_FIX` branches there is a `checkSlow`, compiled out in Shipping. Zeroed weak-ptr is safe **either way the macro resolves** — which removes that uncertainty entirely, and is also what forces `LoadSynchronous` down the resolve/load path (`SoftObjectPtr.h:52-63`: `Get()` returns null → `ToSoftObjectPath().TryLoad()`).\n\nCan `SubPathString` stay empty for our target? **Yes, guaranteed by the parser.** `FSoftObjectPath::SetPath(FWideStringView)` splits on `FAsciiSet(\".\") + SUBOBJECT_DELIMITER_CHAR` and takes the branch `SoftObjectPath.cpp:147-154`:\n```\nFWideStringView AssetNameView = FAsciiSet::FindPrefixWithout(Path, Delimiters);\nif (AssetNameView.Len() == Path.Len())\n{\n    // No subobject path\n    AssetPath = FTopLevelAssetPath(FName(PackageNameView), FName(AssetNameView));\n    SubPathString.Empty();\n    return;\n}\n```\n`/Game/ModKit/MK_Canary.MK_Canary` has one `.` and no `:`, so it lands here and `SubPathString` is left empty. If we ever needed a *non-empty* `SubPathString`, that would be a genuine blocker (we'd be handed an engine-`FMemory::Malloc`'d buffer we cannot free from MinGW) — so **hard-constrain the target path to contain no `:`**.\n\n### Can the FNames be supplied as raw `(ComparisonIndex, Number)` pairs? — Yes *mechanically*, but see §3\n\nStructurally, yes: `FName` is two `uint32`s and nothing else in this build, so writing a `(ComparisonIndex, Number)` pair harvested from the live `FNamePool` via ERI I-03 produces a bit-identical `FName` to one the engine would construct. `Number` is stored as **actual+1** (\"stored internally as 1 more than actual, so zero'd memory will be the default, no-instance case\", `NameTypes.h:1110-1111`), so a plain unnumbered name harvested from the pool must be written with `Number = 0`. That is a real trap for anyone tempted to write `Number = 1`.\n\nThe catch is not the encoding. It is §3.\n\n## 3. The FNames we need do not exist in the game — this IS a real obstacle, and it has a clean way around it\n\n**The finding.** Exact-match counts against `research/evidence/RF-01/global-names.txt` (OBSERVED):\n\n| name | present |\n|---|---|\n| `MK_Canary` | **0** |\n| `ModKit` | **0** |\n| `CT05Row` | **0** |\n| `CT05CANARY8F4A2E1C` | **0** |\n| `CT05MIRROR` | **0** |\n| `MirrorEntryType` | **0** |\n| `MirroredName` | **0** |\n| `MirrorTableRow` | 1 |\n| `DataTable` | 1 |\n| `SoftObjectPath` | 1 |\n\n(`MirrorTableRow` and `DataTable` are present because the RowStruct is the *engine's* `FMirrorTableRow` — `MirrorDataTable.h:43 struct FMirrorTableRow : public FTableRowBase` — and appears at `script-objects.tsv:12570 … /Script/Engine/MirrorTableRow`, with `/Script/Engine/DataTable` at line 7796. Only the names *we* authored are missing. That is exactly the expected pattern and it validates the check.)\n\n**Why it bites.** `FSoftObjectPath` stores the package path as a *single* `FName` covering the whole `/Game/ModKit/MK_Canary` string. There is no way to compose it from existing name entries. A \"find-only\" `FName` lookup for a string not in the pool yields `NAME_None` — and `FSoftObjectPath::IsNull()` is `AssetPath.IsNull()` is `PackageName.IsNone()` (`SoftObjectPath.h:236-239`, `TopLevelAssetPath.h:110-113`). `LoadSynchronous` would then early-out at `if (Asset == nullptr && !IsNull())` (`SoftObjectPtr.h:55`) and return `nullptr` **silently**, with no exception and no log — the worst possible failure mode: it looks like \"the game refused to load our package\" when in fact we never asked. **This alone would have produced a false negative for ESC-02.**\n\n**Why hand-writing pool entries is not the answer.** Inserting into `FNamePool` from a `CreateRemoteThread` thread means replicating `FNamePool`'s shard/lock/block-allocation protocol by hand against a live allocator. That is far outside the \"narrowest and most verifiable\" bar and I do not recommend it.\n\n**The way around it: let the engine create the FNames, via reflection, with a string.** Both required entry points exist in *this* game's reflection data (OBSERVED):\n\n```\nscript-objects.tsv:11836  5fbf4db1648bbd9b  57828571c49eba95  …  /Script/Engine/KismetSystemLibrary/MakeSoftObjectPath\nscript-objects.tsv:11663  5dbc3ae2795c68f9  57828571c49eba95  …  /Script/Engine/KismetSystemLibrary/Conv_SoftObjPathToSoftObjRef\nscript-objects.tsv:11571  4b235206248c780f  54d096799ec6d5fe  …  /Script/Engine/KismetStringLibrary/Conv_StringToName\n```\n```\nKismetSystemLibrary.h:289-291\n\t/** Builds a Soft Object Path struct from a string that contains a full /folder/packagename.object path */\n\tUFUNCTION(BlueprintPure, Category = \"Utilities\", meta = (Keywords = \"construct build\", NativeMakeFunc, BlueprintThreadSafe, BlueprintAutocast))\n\tstatic ENGINE_API FSoftObjectPath MakeSoftObjectPath(const FString& PathString);\n\nKismetSystemLibrary.cpp:1273-1284  → FSoftObjectPath SoftObjectPath(PathString);  (+ a LogRuntimeError if !IsValid)\n```\n`FSoftObjectPath(const FString&)` (`SoftObjectPath.h:51`) → `SetPath(FStringView)` → `FName(PackageNameView)` / `FName(AssetNameView)`, which are **find-or-add** constructors. The engine inserts both names into the live pool itself, on the GameThread, using its own code. That is the whole problem, solved.\n\n**Predicted Parms for `MakeSoftObjectPath` (INFERRED; verify ParmsSize/ReturnValueOffset live the same way):**\n```\n+0x00  PathString  : FStrProperty, 16 bytes   { TCHAR* Data ; int32 Num ; int32 Max }\n+0x10  ReturnValue : FStructProperty(FSoftObjectPath), 32 bytes\n+0x30  == ParmsSize ;  ReturnValueOffset = 0x10\n```\n\n**Ownership analysis for the input FString (this is the load-bearing part):** we supply `{Data = <our own buffer>, ArrayNum = Len+1, ArrayMax = Len+1}` with a UTF-16LE, **null-terminated** string (`ArrayNum` must include the terminator — `FString::Len()` is `Num-1`, `UnrealString.h.inl:1082-1085`).\n- `ProcessEvent` does `FMemory::Memcpy(Frame, Parms, Function->ParmsSize)` (`ScriptCore.cpp:2078-2081`) — a *bitwise* copy; nothing is constructed.\n- The thunk's `P_GET_PROPERTY_REF` path goes through `FFrame::StepCompiledInRef` (`Stack.h:524-543`) → `StepExplicitProperty` (`ScriptCore.cpp:481-505`), which for a non-out param does `CopyCompleteValueToScriptVM_InContainer(Result, Locals)`. That is an `FString` **copy-assign into an engine-allocated temporary** — it *reads* our buffer and mallocs its own. The temporary is destroyed by the thunk.\n- On exit, `ScriptCore.cpp:2145-2157`: for a `DestructorLink` property that *is* inside the parms region and is not `CPF_OutParm`, ProcessEvent **memcpy's the frame copy back over `Parms`** rather than destroying it — \"so the correct copy is destroyed when the event function returns\". Bits identical to what we wrote.\n\nNet: **the engine never calls `FMemory::Free` on our buffer.** We own it (a static array inside the probe DLL is fine) and we simply never hand it to the engine's allocator. This is the one safe way to cross the MinGW↔MSVC boundary with an `FString`, and it works *only* because the parameter is an input.\n\n**Ownership analysis for the returned `FSoftObjectPath`:** ProcessEvent computes `ReturnValueAddress = (uint8*)Parms + Function->ReturnValueOffset` (`ScriptCore.cpp:2138-2141`) — the thunk assigns **directly into our buffer**. `FSoftObjectPath::operator=` runs `FString` copy-assign with our zeroed 16 bytes as the destination, which is legal (empty, null-guarded). And per `SoftObjectPath.cpp:147-154` above, the resulting `SubPathString` is **empty** for our delimiter-free path, so **no engine-allocated memory lands in our buffer.** Confirm this by asserting `Parms[0x10+0x10 .. 0x10+0x1F] == 0` after the call. If it is ever non-zero, **stop** — we would be holding an engine allocation we cannot free.\n\n### Concrete byte-level plan (one GameThread callback, two ProcessEvent calls)\n\nAll three UObject addresses come from ERI's proven NAME→ADDRESS oracle (I-04/I-05); no raw RVA anywhere except the already-proven `ProcessEvent` vtable slot 77.\n\n```\nPRE-FLIGHT (read-only, ERI, before any escalation)\n  resolve /Script/Engine.KismetSystemLibrary, :MakeSoftObjectPath, :LoadAsset_Blocking,\n          /Script/Engine.Default__KismetSystemLibrary\n  assert MakeSoftObjectPath  : NumParms==2, ParmsSize==0x30, ReturnValueOffset==0x10\n  assert LoadAsset_Blocking  : NumParms==2, ParmsSize==0x30, ReturnValueOffset==0x28\n  assert /Script/CoreUObject.SoftObjectPath UScriptStruct PropertiesSize==32   (offset +0x58, INFERRED)\n  ANY mismatch  ->  abort, do not escalate.\n\nSTEP A  (inside the one GameThread callback)\n  static const wchar_t PATH[] = L\"/Game/ModKit/MK_Canary.MK_Canary\";   // 32 chars + NUL, no ':'\n  uint8 A[0x30]; memset(A, 0, 0x30);\n  *(void**)(A+0x00) = (void*)PATH;\n  *(int32*)(A+0x08) = 33;      // Len+1, MUST include terminator\n  *(int32*)(A+0x0C) = 33;\n  ProcessEvent(Default__KismetSystemLibrary, MakeSoftObjectPath, A);   // vtable slot 77\n  // A+0x10 .. A+0x2F now holds a fully-formed FSoftObjectPath\n  assert *(uint32*)(A+0x10) != 0;                  // PackageName.ComparisonIndex non-zero == not NAME_None\n  assert bytes A+0x20 .. A+0x2F are all zero;      // SubPathString empty -> nothing to free\n\nSTEP B  (same callback)\n  uint8 B[0x30]; memset(B, 0, 0x30);\n  memcpy(B+0x08, A+0x10, 32);                      // FSoftObjectPath into TSoftObjectPtr::ObjectID\n                                                   // B+0x00..0x07 stays zero == null FWeakObjectPtr\n  ProcessEvent(Default__KismetSystemLibrary, LoadAsset_Blocking, B);\n  UObject* Result = *(UObject**)(B+0x28);\n\nVERIFY (read-only, ERI, after)\n  Result != nullptr; its NamePrivate decodes to \"MK_Canary\";\n  its ClassPrivate is /Script/Engine.DataTable;\n  /Game/ModKit/MK_Canary now exists as a UPackage in GUObjectArray;\n  the FNames \"/Game/ModKit/MK_Canary\" and \"MK_Canary\" are now in the live FNamePool\n      (they were not before — that transition is itself independent proof the call ran).\n  Leak accounting: exactly zero engine allocations retained by us.\n```\n\nNothing here constructs a C++ object in MinGW-compiled code. Every non-POD value is either all-zero bytes (provably a valid empty `FString` / null `FWeakObjectPtr`) or produced by MSVC-compiled engine code writing into our buffer.\n\nTwo side effects to declare up front, both benign and both irreversible-but-harmless: the live `FNamePool` permanently gains two entries (`FNamePool` never shrinks anyway, and any normal load would do the same), and `MakeSoftObjectPath` calls `LogRuntimeError` if the path fails to parse (`KismetSystemLibrary.cpp:1276-1281`) — the format string is stripped in this build, so expect no visible output, which is why the `ComparisonIndex != 0` assertion carries the check instead.\n\n## 4. Candidate per-frame GameThread UFunctions in this game\n\n### Native declarations present in this game's reflection data (OBSERVED, `script-objects.tsv`)\n\n```\nline  6257: raw_name=\"ReceiveTick\"  function_flags_raw=\"0x8020802\"\n  is_native=false  is_event=true  is_static=false\n  num_parms=1  parms_size=4  return_value_offset=65535\n  parameters=[{name:\"DeltaSeconds\", property_class:\"FFloatProperty\", offset:0, size:4}]\n```\n`0x8020802` = `FUNC_BlueprintEvent | FUNC_Public | FUNC_Event | FUNC_RequiredAPI`. **No `FUNC_Native`** — the base `AActor::ReceiveTick` is a pure Blueprint-event stub, and `Function->Script.Num() == 0` on it means `ProcessEvent` early-returns (`ScriptCore.cpp:2015-2018`) for any actor whose class does not override it.\n\n`research/evidence/CR-01/master-classes-i05-i06.json` — a live BP class that **does** override it:\n```\nBP_MasterHoldable_C :: ReceiveTick\n  address 0x29b1cbddec0  function_flags 134350848 (0x08020800)\n    = FUNC_BlueprintEvent | FUNC_Public | FUNC_Event   (again no FUNC_Native)\n  num_parms 1  parms_size 4  return_value_offset 65535  local_variable_count 0\n `research/evidence/CR-01/semantic-scan-03.json`):\n`/Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Items/HoldableItems/BP_MasterHoldable.BP_MasterHoldable_C`, `live_instance_count = 1`.\n\nThe same class also carries `OnRep_TickEnabled` (flags `0x0C000000` = `FUNC_BlueprintCallable | FUNC_BlueprintEvent`, 0 parms) — a replicated `TickEnabled` bool, which means **this actor's tick is conditional at runtime.** Do not treat it as guaranteed-per-frame.\n\nRelated: `/Game/SmartAI/Blueprints/AI/Holdables/BP_AIMasterHoldable.BP_AIMasterHoldable_C`, `live_instance_count = 1`.\n\n### Live BP classes with large instance counts (OBSERVED, `semantic-scan-03.json`, `class_universe_size = 143387`)\n\nHighest-population classes actually alive in that session — good hunting ground, but **I have not verified any of these implements `ReceiveTick`**, only that instances exist:\n```\n6480  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Components/BP_GridComponent.BP_GridComponent_C\n3041  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Components/BP_GroundCheckComponent.BP_GroundCheckComponent_C\n 631  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Widgets/BP_QuickSlotKey.BP_QuickSlotKey_C\n 613  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Widgets/BP_InventoryItemIcon.BP_InventoryItemIcon_C\n 601  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Widgets/BP_InventoryCell.BP_InventoryCell_C\n 215  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Components/BP_MasterInventory.BP_MasterInventory_C\n   2  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Saving/BP_SGKGameInstance.BP_SGKGameInstance_C\n   2  /Game/SurvivalGameKitV2/Blueprints/Widgets/BP_InventoryHUD.BP_InventoryHUD_C\n```\nThe widget classes are the interesting family for `UUserWidget::Tick` (`script-objects.tsv:32311`), since `UUserWidget::NativeTick` fires from Slate on the GameThread every frame for any widget in the viewport — no `bCanEverTick` gate, no `TickEnabled` replication.\n\nAlso live-observed and game-owned: `/Script/MISERY.MiseryGameViewportClient` (CDO at `script-objects.tsv:22361`, class at 22365-ish; live snapshot shows one reflected UFunction, `SetInjectedScrollBox`, flags `0x4020401`, native, 1 parm). `UGameViewportClient::Tick` is a C++ virtual, not a UFunction, so it is not reachable by reflection — but it is the single most reliably per-frame GameThread call site in the process, and MISERY subclasses it. Worth naming as a vtable-slot candidate for a separate investigation, not as a reflection candidate.\n\n### The blocker on the one-shot-hook family that you should know before designing it\n\nThe obvious narrow one-shot is to swap `UFunction::Func` on a per-frame UFunction, let it fire once, and restore it. **We cannot currently compute that field's offset.** `eri.py:4800-4808` says so explicitly and deliberately refuses to read it:\n\n> \"RPCId/RPCResponseId (+0xBA/+0xBC) and everything after — including every `#if UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` / `#if WITH_LIVE_CODING` conditionally-compiled field and the native Func pointer — are DELIBERATELY never read (real, unresolved conditional-compilation uncertainty this pass does not attempt to resolve)\"\n\nFrom source the defaults are `UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS 1` (`Build.h:363-365`) and `WITH_LIVE_CODING 0` (`Build.h:153-155`), which with the `Class.h:1797-1830` declaration order gives (**INFERRED, NOT VERIFIED**):\n```\n+0xB0 FunctionFlags   +0xB4 NumParms   +0xB5 pad   +0xB6 ParmsSize   +0xB8 ReturnValueOffset\n+0xBA RPCId           +0xBC RPCResponseId          +0xBE pad(2)\n+0xC0 FirstPropertyToInit   +0xC8 EventGraphFunction   +0xD0 EventGraphCallOffset   +0xD4 pad(4)\n+0xD8 Func            sizeof(UFunction) = 0xE0\n```\nBoth are `#ifndef` guards that UBT can override, and this build's `.pdata` gives us no names to check against. **Do not write to `+0xD8` on this claim.** It is verifiable cheaply and read-only first: for a *native* UFunction (e.g. `/Script/MISERY.MiseryBlueprintFunctionLibrary:IsSteamDeck`, already proven callable) read the 8 bytes at `+0xD8` and confirm they land inside the module's `.text` **and** on a `.pdata` RUNTIME_FUNCTION *start* boundary; then confirm that many distinct Blueprint-implemented UFunctions (which all route to `UObject::ProcessInternal`) share one identical value at `+0xD8` while native ones do not. Two independent structural signals, zero writes. Only after that does a Func-swap become a defensible escalation.\n\n`UFunction::Invoke` is `return (*Func)(Obj, Stack, RESULT_PARAM);` (`Class.cpp:6836-6848`), so a swap there is genuinely narrow — one pointer, restored inside the same call. But it is not yet earned.\n\nTwo further design notes for the hook target, from source:\n- `AActor::Tick` reaches `ReceiveTick` only under `if (GetClass()->HasAnyClassFlags(CLASS_CompiledFromBlueprint) || !GetClass()->HasAnyClassFlags(CLASS_Native))` and `GetWorldSettings() != nullptr` (`Actor.cpp:1495-1504`). Native-only actors never call it. BP actors do.\n- `ProcessEvent`'s very first statement is `checkf(!IsUnreachable(), … \"Alternatively, this function was called from a non-GameThread which is unsafe.\")` (`ScriptCore.cpp:1974-1976`) — compiled out in Shipping, which is precisely why the off-thread ESC-01 call did not trip anything. InterlockedCompareExchangePointer(&UF->Func, Orig, &Tramp) — restore FIRST, tolerate failure. The hook is now gone.\n  T3. if (InterlockedCompareExchange(&Fired,1,0) != 0) { Orig(Context, Stack, Result); return; }  // a concurrent or stale entrant: forward, consume nothing.\n  T4. Orig(Context, Stack, Result) — forward, so the Blueprint tick still runs.\n\nDISARM / TIMEOUT, on our thread:\n  D1. Wait for Fired, or a timeout (a few seconds is generous for a per-frame target).\n  D2. On timeout: re-validate the object read-only (ClassPrivate still the UClass named 'Function', NamePrivate still 'ReceiveTick'), then InterlockedCompareExchangePointer(&UF->Func, Orig, &Tramp). The compare value is our unique trampoline address, which is itself a strong 64-bit anti-ABA token — a reused allocation would essentially never hold it at that offset. If the CAS fails, the trampoline already restored; do nothing.\n  D3. NEVER FreeLibrary. A thread can hold &Tramp in a register across our restore, so unmapping is a use-after-unmap crash. But atomicity of THAT store is not the whole safety argument. Both matter.\n\n**(a) `UnrealEditor-CoreUObject.pdb` does not exist on this machine.** OBSERVED: `D:\\Program Files\\UE_5.4\\Engine\\Binaries\\Win64\\` holds **476 `UnrealEditor-*.dll` and 0 `UnrealEditor-*.pdb`**. S-10's own summary agrees: `game-specific-candidate: 0`, `third-party: 554`. RTTI is Tier 5 here, not Tier 2.\n\n---\n\n## THE HEADLINE ANSWER\n\n> **To place a one-shot GameThread callback, which addresses can we obtain with ZERO guessing?**\n\n**Exactly two families, and they are enough to name a target but not enough to schedule it:**\n\n1. **Every native UFunction's entry point** — by name, two independent ways that can be made to cross-check each other byte-for-byte (live `UFunction::Func`; static `FNameNativePtrPair` table). 8,424 distinct names / 9,079 distinct thunk addresses image-wide.\n2. **Every live UObject instance address** — by name, via the already-proven GUObjectArray walk.\n\n**Zero of the thread-dispatch primitives are obtainable this way.** `FTaskGraphInterface::Get()`, `FFunctionGraphTask::CreateAndDispatchWhenReady`, `FTSTicker::GetCoreTicker`, `IsInGameThread`, `GGameThreadId`, `FCoreDelegates::*` are free functions and globals with **no reflection representation, no export, no RTTI, and no surviving log string**. OBSERVED: `IsInGameThread`, `FTSTicker`, `GameThreadTask` → 0 occurrences in either encoding.\n\n**Consequence for GT-01 design:** any mechanism whose address list can be fully derived must be expressible **entirely as (UObject\\*, UFunction\\*) pairs**. The moment a design needs a raw engine free-function address, it re-enters exactly the LOG-0071 failure mode. That is the real constraint this survey produces.\n\n---\n\n## RANKED INVENTORY\n\n### TIER 1 — Reflection as a name→address oracle *(strongest; and stronger than the brief assumed)*\n\n**The mechanism, precisely.** `UFunction::Func` is `FNativeFuncPtr` (`CoreNative.h:17`: `void(*)(UObject* Context, FFrame&, RESULT_DECL)`), declared private in `Class.h:1830`. It is set in `UFunction::Bind()`:\n\n- `Class.cpp:6937-6941` — if **not** `FUNC_Native`: `Func = &UObject::ProcessInternal;`\n- `Class.cpp:6944-6950` — if `FUNC_Native`: looked up by `FName` in `OwnerClass->NativeFunctionLookupTable` and `Func = Found->Pointer;`\n\nThat table is filled by `UClass::AddNativeFunction` (`Class.cpp:6196-6215`, `NativeFunctionLookupTable.Emplace(FName(InName), InPointer)`), fed by `FNativeFunctionRegistrar::RegisterFunctions` from the UHT-generated static array of `FNameNativePtrPair { const char* NameUTF8; FNativeFuncPtr Pointer; }` (`CoreNative.h:20-24`).\n\nSo the chain is: **name (FName) → UFunction object (GUObjectArray walk, already proven I-02..I-06) → read one qword → the native entry point.** No pattern matching, no heuristics, no disassembly. The address is *stored data the engine itself uses to dispatch*, so \"is this the right function?\" is not an inference — if it were wrong, the game itself would be broken.\n\n**Field offset.** Not currently read by ERI — `eri.py` explicitly declines it (\"`native_func_address`/`bytecode_size` stay explicitly null\", comment at `eri.py:4804-4808`). Derivation, from `USTRUCT_TOTAL_SIZE_SHIPPING = 0xB0` which is **live-confirmed** (`eri.py:4797` — I-05 `num_parms_cross_check` = 247 match / 0 mismatch):\n\n| off | field | source |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0xB0 | `FunctionFlags` u32 | Class.h:1797 |\n| 0xB4 / 0xB6 / 0xB8 | `NumParms` u8 / `ParmsSize` u16 / `ReturnValueOffset` u16 | Class.h:1802-1806 |\n| 0xBA / 0xBC | `RPCId` / `RPCResponseId` u16 | Class.h:1808-1810 |\n| 0xC0 | `FirstPropertyToInit` | Class.h:1813 |\n| 0xC8 | `EventGraphFunction` | Class.h:1817, `UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` |\n| 0xD0 | `EventGraphCallOffset` i32 (+4 pad) | Class.h:1820 |\n| — | `SingletonPtr` **absent** | `WITH_LIVE_CODING` = 0, Build.h:153-155 |\n| **0xD8** | **`Func`** | Class.h:1830 |\n\n`UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` defaults to **1** (Build.h:363-365) and OBSERVED: UnrealBuildTool never defines it (grep over `Engine/Source/Programs/UnrealBuildTool/` → empty; only 6 files in the whole tree mention it, none of them build config). So 0xD8 is a confident **INFERRED** value — but it must still be *measured*, not assumed, and the measurement is free (below).\n\n**Two bonus zero-guess addresses this yields:**\n- `&UObject::ProcessInternal` — read `Func` on any non-native UFunction (Class.cpp:6940). Every Blueprint UFunction in the process must yield the *identical* value; thousands of objects agreeing is a self-validating derivation.\n- `ProcessEvent` — already proven, vtable slot 77 (ESC-01).\n\n**What Tier 1 cannot reach:** free functions (`LoadPackage`, `FlushAsyncLoading`), statics, globals (`GEngine` as a symbol — though the *object* is reachable), non-UFunction methods, and anything in a UClass that isn't a UFunction. `ClassConstructor` / `CppClassStaticFunctions` in UClass are reachable *as fields* by the same technique, but their offsets are further out than anything ERI has validated, so they'd need their own derivation.\n\n---\n\n### TIER 2 — The UHT static registration tables *(new; a second, fully independent oracle)*\n\nThis is the finding I did not expect and it is the most useful thing in this report. **The UHT-generated name↔pointer tables survive completely in `.rdata`.**\n\nOBSERVED, image-wide census (`census.py`):\n- **13,677** `Z_Construct_UFunction_*` singleton thunks / **15,310** distinct names, in `FClassFunctionLinkInfo` arrays\n- **9,079** native exec-thunk addresses / **8,424** distinct names, in `FNameNativePtrPair` arrays\n- **overlap between the two address sets: 0**\n\n**This means `LoadAsset_Blocking`'s native entry point is derivable statically, offline, with no live process at all** — and it is the *same value* the live `UFunction::Func` must hold, by Class.cpp:6949. Two oracles, wholly independent derivations, one predicted equality. That is the evidentiary standard LOG-0071 demanded and 0x12CF3B0 never had.\n\n**⚠ There is a real off-by-one trap here and I walked into it before catching it.** The two structs have *opposite* field order:\n- `FNameNativePtrPair { const char* NameUTF8; FNativeFuncPtr Pointer; }` — CoreNative.h:20-24\n- `FClassFunctionLinkInfo { UFunction* (*CreateFuncPtr)(); const char* FuncNameUTF8; }` — **Class.h:2714-2718**\n\nBoth produce a 16-byte stride with a name pointer and a `.text` pointer, so a naive \"find name, read next qword\" scan is **structurally valid on both and wrong on one**. My first pass reported `LoadAsset_Blocking → 0x36c4f00`. That is wrong; 0x36c4f00 is `Z_Construct_..._LoadAssetClass`.\n\n**The disambiguation is exact, not heuristic:** `FClassParams.FunctionLinkArray` (`UObjectGlobals.h:3833`, field index 4, +0x20) names the array base explicitly, and `FClassParams` also carries `NumFunctions` as a bitfield (`UObjectGlobals.h:3839`, bits 4-14).\n\n**Worked example — UKismetSystemLibrary (`fclassparams.py`, `crosstable2.py`), all OBSERVED:**\n\n```\nFClassParams @ rva 0x673cc80   (referenced by lea rdx @ 0x36bad60)\n  ClassNoRegisterFunc  = 0x36ba900\n  FunctionLinkArray    = 0x67386a0\n  bitfield 0x00001142 -> NumFunctions = 276\n```\nReconstructing 276 entries from 0x67386a0 as `{func, name}`:\n- 276/276 targets are `.pdata` function starts\n- **all 276 targets are exactly 47 (0x2f) bytes** — zero variance, all with the singleton prologue `48 83 ec 28 | 48 8b 05 …` (`sub rsp,0x28; mov rax,[rip+X]; test rax,rax; jnz; lea rdx,FuncParams; lea rcx,&Singleton`)\n\nReconstructing the `FNameNativePtrPair` array at base **0x67683d0** (base found by locating the pointer to the alphabetically-first name literal `AddFloatHistorySample`; the qword *before* the base is string data, not a pointer — so the array genuinely starts with a name):\n- 274 entries\n- name sets differ from the 276 by **exactly two**: `OnAssetLoaded__DelegateSignature` and `OnAssetClassLoaded__DelegateSignature` — which are `UDelegateFunction` (Class.h:1956), not native, and therefore correctly absent from a natives table. **The discrepancy is predicted by the source, which is what makes the reconstruction trustworthy.**\n- target address overlap with the `Z_Construct` set: **0**\n\n**Result:**\n```\nLoadAsset_Blocking   Z_Construct = rva 0x36c4c00 (0x2f)\nLoadAsset_Blocking   exec thunk  = rva 0x37445f0 (0x11e)   <-- predicted UFunction::Func\n```\n\n**Independent structural corroboration.** `execIsSteamDeck` (rva 0x3f00100, from `UMiseryBlueprintFunctionLibrary`'s table — the function ESC-01 already called successfully live) contains\n`48 8b 42 20 | 33 c9 | 48 85 c0 | 0f 95 c1 | 48 03 c8 | 48 89 4a 20`\n= `mov rax,[rdx+0x20]; xor ecx,ecx; test rax,rax; setne cl; add rcx,rax; mov [rdx+0x20],rcx`\nwhich is literally **`P_FINISH` → `Stack.Code += !!Stack.Code;`** (`ScriptMacros.h:108`) operating on `FFrame::Code` at +0x20 (FOutputDevice 0x10 + Node 0x10 + Object 0x18 → Code 0x20, `Stack.h:136-142`), with `rdx` = the `FFrame&` second parameter. This is only meaningful for a function with the `FNativeFuncPtr` signature. Honest limit: this exact byte string matches only **~208 functions image-wide**, so it corroborates *when present* and proves nothing when absent (`execLoadAsset_Blocking` does not contain it — different register allocation).\n\n**Caveat — ICF makes address→name non-injective.** OBSERVED in UKismetSystemLibrary alone: **18 exec-thunk addresses shared by 42 names** (e.g. `0x3714b30` ← `BreakSoftClassPath`, `BreakSoftObjectPath`; `0x2ca9fe0` ← 5 names). `name → address` is a function; `address → name` is not. Never reason backwards from an address to a name.\n\n---\n\n### TIER 3 — `.pdata` RUNTIME_FUNCTION boundaries *(validator only — and weaker than assumed)*\n\nOBSERVED: 411,385 records, **411,385 unique starts, 100% inside `.text`** (`.text` rva 0x1000–0x5dcaa9c). Sizes min 1 / median 83 / max 143,329.\n\nCorrect use: \"candidate address X is a function start\" is a **necessary-ish** condition, cheap, and would have flagged nothing about 0x12CF3B0 (which *is* a valid start — .pdata confirmed it and it still wasn't LoadPackage). It is a sanity filter, never identity.\n\n**Important correction to the brief's framing:** `.pdata` does **not** cover every function. OBSERVED (`leaf.py`): **11 of 274** UKismetSystemLibrary exec thunks (4.0%) have **no `.pdata` record** — `CollectGarbage`, `GetFrameCount`, `IsScreensaverEnabled`, `RegisterForRemoteNotifications`, … Every one of them begins with the bare `P_FINISH` sequence and nothing else: they are true x64 leaf functions with no prologue, so MSVC legitimately omits unwind data. **So \"not a .pdata start\" must never be used to reject a candidate.** Using it as a rejection test would have thrown away 11 correct addresses in this one class.\n\n---\n\n### TIER 4 — Cross-build symbol transplant *(credible for a narrow slice, a trap in general)*\n\nThe Editor-PDB route as written is unavailable (§0a). The *available* route is materially better than the brief assumed and still limited:\n\n`UnrealPak-CoreUObject.pdb` / `ShaderCompileWorker-CoreUObject.pdb` are **Program targets**, i.e. `WITH_EDITOR=0` and `WITH_EDITORONLY_DATA=0` — the same conditional-compilation regime as the game, which is exactly what governs UE struct layouts and function bodies. That removes the single largest source of divergence the brief was worried about.\n\nWhat still diverges, and it is a lot:\n- **Development vs Shipping** — different inlining, different `checkf`/`ensure` expansion, `UE_BUILD_SHIPPING` gates all over the load path\n- **Modular DLL vs monolithic** — cross-module calls become direct calls; LTCG/`/OPT:ICF` across the whole 98 MB `.text` reshapes and *merges* bodies (we measured ICF folding actively)\n- **`USE_COMPILED_IN_NATIVES`, `STATS`, `CSV_PROFILER`, `UE_TRACE_ENABLED`** all differ\n\nHonest verdict: usable for **layout and structural questions** (confirming a field offset, confirming a struct's shape) — genuinely helpful and low-risk there. **Not** usable to transplant a byte signature and declare a function identity. A signature that matches is weak evidence; a signature that fails to match is no evidence at all. For GT-01 it is strictly worse than Tiers 1-2, which give the answer outright. **Do not spend the session on it.**\n\n---\n\n### TIER 5 — Surviving strings *(count-based, as requested)*\n\nOBSERVED, `.rdata` (`strscan.py`): **62,651** UTF-16LE strings ≥4 chars, **140,161** ASCII strings ≥4 chars.\n\n| class | count | note |\n|---|---|---|\n| `/Script/…` (UTF-16) | 439 | of which **266** are exact `/Script/Module.Class`, **172** module-only |\n| `/Engine/…` (UTF-16) | 494 | asset paths |\n| `/Game/…` (UTF-16) | 2 | essentially nothing |\n| other mount paths | 125 | |\n| `.cpp` / `.h` ANSI `__FILE__` literals | 393 / 160 | present but, per LOG-0071, not `lea`-referenced |\n| `.?A*` RTTI names | 636 + 23 | all third-party (§0b) |\n| dotted `Foo.Bar[.Baz]` UTF-16 | **7,110** | see below |\n| `LoadAsset_Blocking` | **1 ASCII, 0 UTF-16** | the UHT registration literal — Tier 2 |\n| `net.CheckNoLoadPackages` / `s.AsyncLoadingThreadEnabled` / `gc.MaxObjectsInGame` | 1 each, UTF-16 | cvar names survive |\n| `IsInGameThread`, `FTSTicker`, `GameThreadTask`, `MK_Canary` | **0** | |\n\n**The dotted family is a real, previously-unused anchor set.** OBSERVED (`anchors.py`, full `lea`-rip index over `.text` = 256,225 distinct targets): of 7,110 dotted UTF-16 literals, **6,436 have exactly one `.text` LEA xref**, landing in 5,088 distinct functions, of which **4,890 hold exactly one such literal — a 1:1 string→function anchor.** Spot-checked examples resolve to `FAutoConsoleVariableRef` dynamic initializers: `TaskGraph.NumWorkerThreadsToIgnore` → func 0xcc99e4, `TaskGraph.TaskThreadPriority` → 0xcca184, `Async.ParallelFor.YieldingTimeout` → 0xcc9b80.\n\nThis is precisely the \"string → LEA xref → function body\" technique LOG-0071 correctly found *unavailable for LoadPackage*. It **is** available for ~4,890 other functions. Two honest limits: (i) it anchors the *cvar registrar*, and reaching a *consumer* requires following the cvar object's storage through further xrefs — a multi-step inference chain, not a one-step derivation; (ii) I did **not** determine the producer of the whole 7,110 set — entries like `Actor.AddActorLocalOffset` do not look like cvars and I am not going to guess what emits them. Treat this tier as **a genuine lead worth one scoped experiment**, not as an established capability. It is not needed for GT-01.\n\n---\n\n### TIER 6 — Imports / IAT *(no engine value)*\n\nOBSERVED: 38 DLLs, 937 imports. `KERNEL32` 252, `USER32` 143, `MSVCP140` 107, UCRT split across `api-ms-win-crt-*`, `WS2_32` 43, `VCRUNTIME140` 27+1. You could reach e.g. `TlsGetValue` and in principle chase thread-local state, but nothing in the engine's dispatch layer is anchorable this way. Worth exactly one thing: **it is a clean way to get `LoadLibraryW`/`GetProcAddress`-adjacent Win32 addresses inside the target**, which the injection mechanics already have by other means.\n\n---\n\n## THE ONE MEASUREMENT I RECOMMEND NEXT — read-only, inside ERI's proven envelope\n\nDo **not** assume `Func` is at 0xD8. **Measure it**, with the static table as the answer key:\n\n1. Offline (already done, reproducible): from the `FNameNativePtrPair` arrays, build `name → predicted thunk RVA` for a few hundred native UFunctions across several classes.\n2. Live, `ReadProcessMemory` only (extends existing I-05, no new capability class): for each of those UFunctions found by the existing GUObjectArray walk, **sweep candidate offsets 0xB8…0x120 in 8-byte steps** and record which offset reproduces the static prediction.\n3. Accept the offset only if **one single offset matches for ~100% of the sample and every other offset matches ~0%.**\n\nThat test is self-validating in both directions: it pins the offset *and* it confirms the static-table reconstruction, from two derivations that share no intermediate value. If 0xD8 is right you will see it; if `UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` turns out to be 0 you will see 0xC8 instead and learn that too. Either way the guessing count is zero.\n\nAlso worth capturing in the same pass, free: read `Func` on a few thousand *non-native* UFunctions and confirm they all hold one identical value — that value is `&UObject::ProcessInternal`, derived with zero guessing and validated by mass agreement.\n\n**Then, and only then**, `/Script/Engine.KismetSystemLibrary → LoadAsset_Blocking → Func` is an address with a reproducible derivation and an independent offline cross-check — which is exactly what ESC-02 lacked, and the reason it was correctly stopped.\n\n**Still unsolved after all of the above, and I want to be blunt about it:** none of this gets you *onto* the GameThread. It cannot carry the LoadAsset_Blocking payload, and neither can any other member of this family (see rationale).", "best_candidate_rationale": "Why it wins inside the family: it is the only reflection-only route whose entire on-our-thread footprint is (a) one ProcessEvent of one static UFunction and (b) a lock-protected insert into the audio chunk cache (FScopeLock at AudioStreamingCache.cpp:788), and whose hand-off to the GameThread is an unconditional AsyncTask(ENamedThreads::GameThread) (AudioMixerBlueprintLibrary.cpp:621) that fires on every branch including the failure branches. It needs no NewObject, no unlocked container mutation, no FString/TArray/FSoftObjectPath fabrication (both parms are POD: a raw UObject* and a 16-byte TScriptDelegate), no raw RVA beyond the already-proven ProcessEvent vtable slot 77, and it installs no hook, so there is nothing to deactivate. It runs the callback exactly once, its blast radius is one LRU-evictable audio chunk, and it does no package loading — which matches the owner's 'first a test callback that does NO package loading' constraint exactly. Proof of GameThread execution is sound by construction: the AsyncTask GameThread queue has exactly one executor.\n\nTHE DECISIVE NEGATIVE, and the most important result of this investigation: NO reflection-only route in this family can deliver the second half of the owner's plan. Every one of these routes delivers control by NAME to a pre-existing UFunction through TScriptDelegate::ProcessDelegate, which does `Function = ObjectPtr->FindFunctionChecked(FunctionName); ObjectPtr->ProcessEvent(Function, Parameters)` (ScriptDelegates.h:443-447) where `Parameters` is a stack struct laid out for the DELEGATE's signature, while ProcessEvent memcpys `Function->ParmsSize` bytes out of it (ScriptCore.cpp:2081-2084). Two consequences:\n  1. We do not choose the callee's arguments — the engine supplies them at fire time. We can only choose WHICH pre-existing UFunction runs.\n  2. If the callee's ParmsSize exceeds the delegate's struct, ProcessEvent overreads the engine's stack into the callee's parms. LoadAsset_Blocking takes a TSoftObjectPtr<UObject> (FSoftObjectPath = FTopLevelAssetPath + FString SubPathString, SoftObjectPath.h:341,344, plus TPersistentObjectPtr's cached FWeakObjectPtr) and returns a UObject*, so its ParmsSize is several times any candidate dynamic delegate's. Binding it as a delegate target would overread and then resolve a garbage FSoftObjectPath. That is exactly the class of confidently-wrong move this project already caught once.\nTherefore the only delegate targets that are memory-safe here are ZERO-PARAMETER UFunctions (ParmsSize == 0 makes the memcpy a no-op). The live reflection dump for this exact build already contains two: MiseryBlueprintFunctionLibrary::InitFocusRule (static, num_parms 0, parms_size 0) and MiseryEditableText::EnterEditMode / CommitEditing (instance, parms_size 0) — see research/reflection/misery-24953925-ue5.4.4-bace50f7185d/functions.jsonl.\n\nSo the honest recommendation is a SPLIT: use PrimeSoundForPlayback (or nothing at all) to answer GT-01's 'can we get one pre-chosen UFunction to run on the GameThread', and treat the LoadAsset_Blocking step as requiring a mechanism where WE supply the code that executes there — i.e. the owner-authorised one-shot hook on a known-GameThread callback. Reflection-only scheduling cannot bridge that gap, and stretching it to try would mean handing a mismatched Parms buffer to a UFunction, which is worse than the hook.", "disqualified": ["UKismetSystemLibrary::K2_SetTimer / K2_SetTimerDelegate / K2_SetTimerForNextTick / K2_SetTimerForNextTickDelegate — the callback lands on the GameThread (LevelTick.cpp:1563) but FTimerManager's enqueue is explicitly non-threadsafe (TimerManager.cpp:473-474 and 532-533 both read `// not currently threadsafe` + `check(IsInGameThread())`), there is ZERO locking in the entire class (grep for CriticalSection/FScopeLock/Atomic over TimerManager.cpp and TimerManager.h: no hits), and DO_CHECK==0 in Shipping (Build.h:195-196, 290-291) means the guard is compiled out so it races silently on a TSparseArray + TArray heap + TMap + TSet that the GameThread is concurrently reallocating.", "UKismetSystemLibrary::LoadAsset / LoadAssetClass (latent) — FLoadAssetActionBase's constructor calls RequestAsyncLoad on the CALLING thread (KismetSystemLibrary.cpp:2989) which hits `check(IsInGameThread())` at StreamableManager.cpp:1282; The UE source chain for E1 is real and unconditional: LaunchEngineLoop.cpp:2278-2282 (GGameThreadId, GIsGameThreadIdInitialized, then FPlatformProcess::SetupGameThread), WindowsPlatformProcess.cpp:2327-2330 (SetupGameThread == SetThreadName(TEXT(\"GameThread\"))), :2445-2452 (SetThreadName calls WindowsPlatformProcessImpl::SetThreadDescription unconditionally, not under any !UE_BUILD_SHIPPING guard), :2423-2440 (GetProcAddress on kernel32). Uniqueness holds engine-wide: the only other OS-level namer is WindowsRunnableThread.cpp:62 (per-worker distinct names) and WebBrowserSingleton.cpp:364 (re-sets to GameThread while already on the GT).\n\nBut the recommendation is not \"E1/E2/E3 are good external oracles\" — it is the *conjunction plus the independence argument*, and that central claim is false in three separate, load-bearing ways, two of which I verified by direct observation.\n\n(1) A2 and A3 are the SAME read, not independent mechanisms. I disassembled the actual export: kernel32!GetCurrentThreadId at RVA 0x18620 is `65 48 8B 04 25 48 00 00 00 C3` = `mov rax, gs:[0x48]; ret` (identical bytes in KernelBase.dll at RVA 0xA3230). So I1 = *(TEB+0x48) and I2 = TEB base, both reached through the same GS segment base; and phase 0d *itself* establishes the TID<->TebBase bijection by reading that very field (TEB+0x48) and requiring it equal TID_E1. A2 and A3 therefore cannot disagree except under a kernel-level lie that corrupts both. The analysis states the opposite explicitly — \"Neither uses GetCurrentThreadId, so I1 and I2/I3 can disagree — which is what makes the agreement rule informative rather than tautological\" — and that sentence is refuted by observed bytes. A4 is only marginally better: StackLimit/StackBase come from TEB+0x08/+0x10 of the same TEB. The claimed four-way independence collapses to roughly one-and-a-half observables.\n\n(2) The oracle proves the wrong proposition for phase 2, and the analysis's stated bridge is false. I confirmed IsInGameThread() at ThreadingBase.cpp:177-198: in Shipping the TID comparison is inside `#if !UE_BUILD_SHIPPING && !UE_BUILD_TEST` and the return value is the thread_local tag test `FTaskTagScope::IsCurrentTag(ETaskTag::EGameThread)`. The analysis then argues the tag \"is inherited automatically because the scope wraps the whole tick loop\". It is not. CoreGlobals.h:680 defines `FTaskTagScope(ETaskTag InTag) : FTaskTagScope(false, InTag)` — TagOnlyIfNone == **false** — and ThreadingBase.cpp:120-124 does `ParentTag = ActiveTaskTag; if (!TagOnlyIfNone || ...) { ActiveTaskTag = Tag; }`, i.e. an unconditional overwrite. And EParallelGameThread = EGameThread|EParallelThread = 0x102 != EGameThread = 0x2 (CoreGlobals.h:619-637), while IsCurrentTag is exact equality (ThreadingBase.cpp:163-166). There are real sites that open such a scope on the GameThread OS thread: PSOPrecacheMaterial.cpp:499, Streaming/RenderAssetUpdate.cpp:292, Particles/ParticleSystemManager.cpp:117 (all `FTaskTagScope(ETaskTag::EParallelGameThread)`). For the dynamic extent of those scopes, IsInGameThread() returns FALSE on the GameThread OS thread in this Shipping build. The oracle, which is purely OS-thread-identity, would report PASS. Worse, this is exactly the \"check compiled out in Shipping hides the divergence\" pattern: in Development the `ensureMsgf(oldValue == newValue)` fires and then does `newValue = oldValue;` — so IsInGameThread() has *different semantics* in Development vs Shipping, and the oracle matches the Development semantics. Authorizing a LoadAsset_Blocking on the strength of A1-A6 is therefore authorizing it on a weaker property than the engine's own predicate.\n\n(3) The internal recorder as specified can crash the GameThread. The record block reserves +0x28 for `__builtin_return_address(1)` and the design explicitly anticipates needing it (\"if the mechanism dispatches through a naked thunk we authored, ... capture __builtin_return_address(1) as well\"), while grading I4's abi_hazard as \"None\". GCC's own documentation says a nonzero argument \"can have unpredictable effects, including crashing the calling program\"; on x86-64 GCC implements it as a frame-pointer-chain walk. The caller here is MSVC engine code, and I confirmed from UBT that MISERY is built with frame pointers omitted: TargetRules.cs:2077 `bOmitFramePointers { get; set; } = true`, CppCompileEnvironment.cs:453 same default, and VCToolChain.cs:709-711 emits `/Oy-` only when it is false. That is a structural fingerprint, not a guess.\n0x140f4ded0 is a .pdata function START (411,385 records parsed; record 0x140f4ded0-0x140f4dee9, a 25-byte prologue-only chained record: `mov rax,rsp / push rbp,rsi,r14 / lea rbp,[rax-0x57] / sub rsp,0x90 / mov rsi,[rbp+0x7f] / mov r14,rdx` - rsi = the 5th stack argument, i.e. the functor pointer; r14 = the sret buffer).\nThis is the same class of derivation - multiple independent surviving literals converging on one call target - that the prior analysis itself accepted as rigorous for FTaskGraphInterface::Startup. So \"there is no rigorous, project-permitted way to obtain a callable address; only signature guessing, which is forbidden\" is simply not true for AddTicker/GetCoreTicker. I am NOT authorizing a call to these addresses; they need independent replication and a decision record. But the analysis's central negative claim is unsupported.\n\nDEFECT 2 - UNSTATED, DECISIVE: ASYNCHRONOUS CALLBACK vs. DLL UNLOAD. This is the hazard that actually kills the mechanism and the prior analysis never mentions it. AddTicker only ENQUEUES. The callback body, the TFunction thunk, and the functor's destructor are all code inside the injected probe DLL, and they are executed by the GAME THREAD at an unspecified later frame. The proven injection recipe ends with FreeLibrary. Ticker.h:40 states outright: \"Resources used by the delegate must be released on the ticking thread.\" Confirmed in code: after Fire() returns false the element is not re-added and is destroyed when Ticker.cpp:131 does `Elements.Reset()` - on the game thread - which runs the FTickerDelegate destructor and therefore our functor's destructor. There is no engine-provided completion signal, so the injected thread cannot know when it is safe to unload. Unload too early and the game thread jumps into unmapped memory. This is categorically unlike the PROVEN ProcessEvent call, which is synchronous and fully completed before the injected thread returns. The prior analysis treated FTSTicker as if it inherited ProcessEvent's safety profile; it does not.\n\nDEFECT 3 - BLAST RADIUS IS BADLY UNDERSTATED (\"Small\"). MpscQueue.h:54-55 is a two-instruction publish: `Head.exchange(New)` then `Prev->Next.store(New)`. Between those two stores the list is DETACHED. If the producer thread stalls, is terminated, or is unwound by the VEH guard in that window, the consumer (game thread) sees Tail->Next == nullptr forever and EVERY subsequent AddTicker on the core ticker - engine-wide, for the remaining process lifetime - silently never fires (pak reader retirement, HTTP, backgroundable ticker, etc.). No crash, no log, silent permanent wedge. Our enqueue would run on a CreateRemoteThread thread under a VEH guard whose entire purpose is to catch a fault and abandon. That is precisely the failure mode this queue cannot tolerate.\n\nDEFECT 4 - \"enqueue_threadsafe: YES\" IS AN OVERCLAIM. Ticker.cpp:15 and :22 read `CurrentTime` to compute FireTime. `CurrentTime` is a plain non-atomic double member (Ticker.h:126) that the game thread WRITES at Ticker.cpp:78 (`CurrentTime += DeltaTime`). A foreign-thread AddTicker therefore performs an unsynchronized read concurrently with a write - a formal data race / UB, notwithstanding the header's \"Can be called concurrently\". On x64 an aligned 8-byte load will not tear, and with InDelay=0 a stale value only yields a FireTime slightly in the past (fires immediately), so this is benign IN PRACTICE - but the analysis asserted unqualified thread safety and did not notice the race. Reporting it as an overclaim, not as a kill.\n\nDEFECT 5 - STATE MUTATED AND NOT RESTORED. The return value FDelegateHandle = TWeakPtr<FElement> (Ticker.h:32, default ESPMode::ThreadSafe per SharedPointerFwd.h:24-25) is constructed by the callee into the caller-owned sret buffer with the weak reference count already incremented, and the CALLER owns its destruction. MinGW-side code that simply discards those 16 bytes never runs the MSVC destructor, so the weak count never returns to zero and the FElement's reference-controller block is leaked permanently. Small (tens of bytes) but it is a real un-restored mutation the analysis claimed did not exist (\"No persistent hook, no engine state mutated beyond one heap alloc/free and one queue node\").\n\nDEFECT 6 - MINOR, THE CHECKS THAT WOULD CATCH A BAD ELEMENT ARE GONE. Ticker.cpp:63, 91, 115, 123 are all checkf, compiled out in Shipping (Build.h:313/195-196). A malformed FElement enqueued by us would not be caught at Ticker.cpp:63; Tick would proceed straight to Fire() at Ticker.cpp:110. The absence of \"Invalid state %u\" in the exe is therefore evidence of compile-out, not of string stripping - and it means the mechanism has no defensive net on the game thread.\n\nWHAT I COULD NOT REFUTE: the ABI blocker. It is real and I confirmed its shape in the binary, not just in the header. Arg5 at every call site is a pointer to a stack-constructed object into which the caller stores a RIP-relative pointer (the MSVC-emitted TFunction_OwnedObject vtable, e.g. `lea rcx,[rip+0x3898]; mov [rsp+0x50],rcx` at the FBackgroundableTicker site) plus the captured state - exactly the TFunctionStorage/IFunction_OwnedObject contract of Function.h:120-296 with a 32-byte inline buffer (CoreDefines.h:61). MinGW g++ cannot emit that object; synthesizing a fake vtable would require guessing MSVC's slot order, which the project forbids. Note the FTickerDelegate overload (Ticker.cpp:13) is no escape: FTickerDelegate is a TBaseDelegate with its own MSVC-side storage/allocation contract.\n\nNET: verdict \"disqualified\" stands, but the record must be corrected. The analysis states \"writing into existing TArray slack dodges the array realloc (RemoveAt keeps Max)\". OBSERVED: false. Array.h:1778 defaults AllowShrinking to Yes and Array.h:1785-1788 calls ResizeShrink. ContainerAllocationPolicies.h:127 shrinks to zero whenever NumElements is 0. ContainerAllocationPolicies.h:494-495 then calls FMemory::Realloc because Data is non-null. The array frees its buffer at the end of every drain. This inverts the mitigation from \"partial\" to \"unavailable\": a steady-state DeferredCommands has no allocation at all, so an appender must supply the element buffer too. The allocator blocker is therefore unconditional and doubled, and the analysis's own summary of it (\"Writing into existing TArray slack dodges the array realloc but not the FString buffer free\") is wrong on both halves of the sentence.\n\nB. Proof of execution. This is the claim I attacked hardest because point 2 of the rationale is the candidate's headline advantage. \"Proven by construction\" is a conditional: IF it ran THEN it ran on the GameThread. The analysis never establishes the antecedent. I traced Ar to *GLog and then checked whether GLog produces anything readable in this build. Build.h:306/328 make NO_LOGGING the negation of USE_LOGGING_IN_SHIPPING, which defaults to 0 at Build.h:191-192; the project's established fact that UE_LOG format strings are stripped is exactly the signature of that. I then checked the filesystem read-only: %LOCALAPPDATA%\\MISERY\\Saved\\Logs is present and empty, while Paks, SaveGames, Crashes, Config and a compiled PSO cache are all present — the game has run and produced no log. So there is no channel through which Ar.Logf output, or a \"command not recognized\" result, can be observed. Polling Num() from outside observes the engine's own housekeeping, not our effect. Compare with a real callback, which can write its own GetCurrentThreadId into a shared page — strictly more informative. Point 2 is backwards.\n\nC. Requirement mismatch. Even granting a perfect GMalloc and a perfect enqueue, what lands on the GameThread is an engine exec handler, not a pre-chosen callback of ours. Stage 1 of GT-01 asks for a test callback. This mechanism structurally cannot host one. Ranking it \"best-in-family for stage 1\" upgrades a non-candidate.\n\nD. Blast radius. Rated \"small in principle\". OBSERVED: dispatch is conditional on GetDebugLocalPlayer() (UnrealEngine.cpp:2344), whose body (UnrealEngine.cpp:13525-13539) iterates WorldList and returns the first GameInstance's first player. Whether it returns non-null depends on live world state at drain time and cannot be predicted from a static analysis. If non-null, we enter ULocalPlayer::Exec (LocalPlayer.cpp:1480), which falls through to ViewportClient->Exec and then Super::Exec == UPlayer::Exec (Player.cpp:95), which fans out through World->Exec, PlayerInput->ProcessConsoleExec, PlayerController->ProcessConsoleExec, Pawn, HUD — i.e. into MISERY's own game code, whose FUNC_Exec UFUNCTION set nobody has enumerated. The analysis's \"one extra engine exec command runs for one frame\" assumes a handler set that is not bounded. Player.cpp:115-116's check(World) and check(InWorld == nullptr || InWorld == World) are compiled out at DO_CHECK == 0, so a world mismatch proceeds silently rather than trapping.\n\nE. Reversibility in the failure case. This is where the \"zero residual state\" headline actually fails, and it is the lens I was asked to apply. INFERRED, but every step is source-grounded. Publishing an append is a three-field non-atomic store (Data, ArrayNum, ArrayMax) into a live UObject with no lock. Consider the interleaving where the GameThread snapshots after our ArrayNum store but before our ArrayMax store: RemoveAt(0,1) runs with ArrayNum == 1 and ArrayMax == 0. The FScopeLock at :788 is held continuously through InsertChunk (which re-enters the same recursive FCriticalSection at :1535), through TrimMemory (:1141), and through KickOffAsyncLoad (:841). Inside KickOffAsyncLoad the non-editor path executes `if (CacheElement->IsLoadInProgress()) CacheElement->WaitForAsyncLoadCompletion(true);` (AudioStreamingCache.cpp:1834-1837), which per AudioStreamingCache.h:318-343 CANCELS another subsystem's in-flight bulk-data read and then calls LocalReadRequest->WaitCompletion() — an unbounded blocking wait on disk. So our CreateRemoteThread thread can sit blocked on IO while holding a lock that the GameThread and audio thread take on every GetChunk / AddOrTouchChunk / TrimMemory. If that thread is suspended, our VEH guard trips, or the DLL is unloaded / the thread torn down at the wrong instant, the audio and game threads deadlock permanently. Lock-protected is not the same as safe-to-call-from-a-thread-we-intend-to-tear-down, and the cancelled load is unrestored state.\n\nTwo further defects that make a negative result uninterpretable (so this is arguably the LEAST verifiable route in the family, not the most):\n\n(4) The delegate may be impossible to fabricate from outside for the chosen target, failing SILENTLY. FWeakObjectPtr::Internal_GetObjectItem() returns nullptr whenever ObjectSerialNumber == 0 (WeakObjectPtr.h:269-278), and otherwise demands exact equality with FUObjectItem::SerialNumber (:257-263). That field is LAZILY allocated: FUObjectArray::AllocateSerialNumber (UObjectArray.cpp:434-457) assigns from the global PrimarySerialNumber counter only the first time someone takes a weak pointer to the object; until then it is 0 (UObjectArray.h:48-50, 108-111). ExecuteIfBound → IsBound_Internal (ScriptDelegates.h:158-168) → Object.Get() → nullptr → no-op, no diagnostic. So the route only works if the chosen callback object ALREADY carries a nonzero serial number — unknown, and quite plausibly false for a CDO like Default__MiseryBlueprintFunctionLibrary. Workarounds are all bad: pick a different object by luck; WRITE a serial number into GUObjectArray ourselves (unrestored mutation of engine object metadata, desynchronised from PrimarySerialNumber, with an index-reuse collision hazard after GC); or call AllocateSerialNumber via a raw RVA, which is forbidden. The analysis said \"nothing has to be constructed\" — unsupported. Honest caveat: this is a CONDITIONAL failure and is checkable read-only with ERI before committing; it is an unverified precondition presented as free, not a proven blocker.\n\n(5) A third silent-no-op path the analysis missed entirely. AddOrTouchChunk:792-806: if FindElementForKey finds an element that exists but has bIsLoaded == false (a load already in flight), it calls TouchElement and returns FoundElement->CacheLookupID WITHOUT ever invoking OnLoadCompleted. The callback is dropped on the floor. Combined with the GetCacheForWave-returns-nullptr path when GetNumChunks() <= 1 (:601-615, its guarding ensure compiled out via USE_ENSURES_IN_SHIPPING = 0) and with (4), there are at least three mutually indistinguishable silent-no-op modes. (1) The trampoline's FIRST real act (T2) is InterlockedCompareExchangePointer restoring Func, so the hook lives for at most one dispatch, not one frame. The payload therefore executes exactly once even if the trampoline is ENTERED more than once, and the entry count is measured rather than assumed. FFrame& is a plain pointer; RESULT_DECL is void* const. We construct/copy/destroy NO C++ object — no FString/TArray/TFunction/TDelegate/TSharedPtr/FFrame, no temporaries — exactly the plain-pointer profile of the proven ProcessEvent call, categorically unlike the disqualified TFunction-by-value families. Build with -fno-exceptions -fno-rtti, no global ctors, small frame (avoid __chkstk_ms); verify the built DLL actually emits .pdata/.xdata so an engine stack-walk through our frame does not fault. Use __builtin_return_address(0) ONLY — level 1 walks the RBP chain and MSVC /O2 engine callers are frame-pointer-omitted, so level 1 is an access-violation primitive. Keep the trampoline free of any thread_local/emutls touch: the GameThread predates our DLL load, DLL_THREAD_ATTACH never ran for it, so a TLS access could lock/allocate on the GameThread. PHASE 2 note: LoadAsset_Blocking takes TSoftObjectPtr<UObject> BY VALUE, whose only non-POD member is FString SubPathString — provably a valid empty FString as zeroed memory (null-guarded dtor, Len()==0), and empty by construction for a path with no ':' delimiter.
