Findings below.\n\n---\n\n## What I checked and found SOUND (not vague praise — specific verifications)\n\n- **The dispatch anchor is correct.** For a ProcessEvent-dispatched BP event, `UObject::ProcessEvent` calls `Function->Invoke(this, NewStack, ReturnValueAddress)` (ScriptCore.cpp:2141), and `UFunction::Invoke` executes `return (*Func)(Obj, Stack, RESULT_PARAM);` (Class.cpp:6836/6847). So `UFunction::Func` IS the swap point, and `__builtin_return_address(0)` from the trampoline genuinely lands in `UFunction::Invoke`. Method_2(b)'s provenance claim is source-accurate. (Note: there are *other* `(*Func)` sites — execCallMathFunction ScriptCore.cpp:1075, the EX_CallMath opcode path — but the ProcessEvent/tick route is unambiguously through Invoke. Verified they don't collide.)\n- **`Func == &UObject::ProcessInternal` for BP functions is real** (Class.cpp:6940), so the \"Orig == shared value across thousands of BP UFunctions\" acceptance check is grounded, not hopeful.\n- **E1 is sound.** `FWindowsPlatformProcess::SetupGameThread()` → `SetThreadName(TEXT(\"GameThread\"))` (WindowsPlatformProcess.cpp:2329), and `SetThreadName` calls the real `SetThreadDescription` (2445-2451), which is what `GetThreadDescription` reads back. The game thread is queryably named. Good.\n- **The `Script.Num()==0` early-return gate (ScriptCore.cpp:2015) and the EventGraphFunction fast-call (2041-2049) are exactly where the design says.** Its diligence on those two branches is real.\n\n---\n\n## Prioritized gaps\n\n### G1 — Entire mechanism families were never considered; the \"narrowest\" claim is undefended against them. (highest priority)\nThe rejected list is thorough *within* the five families it enumerates, but three or four families are simply absent, and at least two are plausibly **narrower** than the recommended 8-byte heap write:\n\n- **Hardware-breakpoint (debug register) + VEH, gated on the GameThread TID.** Set `Dr0` = ProcessEvent's address — which is *already a verified code address* from ESC-01 (slot 77 was called successfully, so it is not a LOG-0071 unverified RVA) — via `SetThreadContext` on **only** the GameThread (debug registers are per-thread, so only that thread traps). Install a VEH that gates on `GetCurrentThreadId()==TID_E1`, records POD, clears `Dr0` (one-shot), returns `EXCEPTION_CONTINUE_EXECUTION`. This writes **zero** bytes to engine memory — narrower than 8 bytes on the \"engine mutation footprint\" axis the design uses to argue narrowest. Never mentioned.\n- **`SuspendThread` + `SetThreadContext` RIP redirect.** Redirect the GameThread's RIP to our own stub (save context, run POD payload, restore). Zero engine bytes written, no code address needed at all, inherently one-shot, and proving GameThread execution is trivial (you picked the thread). For **phase-1 GT-01 specifically** (POD-only, no loading) this is dramatically narrower and *skips the entire unmeasured-Func-offset gate that can BLOCK the whole design*.\n- **Per-instance vtable clone / vptr swap.** Clone one live object's vtable, redirect slot 77, repoint that object's vptr. Touches one object (8-byte vptr write at offset 0 — no offset measurement needed), not the class-wide shared vtable the design correctly rejected. Dodges the Func-offset uncertainty entirely.\n- **`QueueUserAPC`** deserves at least a one-line rejection (the GameThread almost never enters an alertable wait in a tight loop → the APC starves → the same silent-timeout trap). Its absence is a completeness hole.\n\n**What closes it:** add these four to `rejected_with_reasons`. The honest rejection of the debug-register and context-hijack families is *phase-2 reentrancy* (see G3), not footprint or ABI — and that reason only applies to phase 2, which means for phase-1 GT-01 they are arguably the correct, narrower choice. That reframes the whole \"one mechanism for both phases\" decision (G2).\n\n### G2 — Phase-1/phase-2 coupling is a choice presented as a necessity, and it inflates phase-1 risk.\nThe design couples GT-01's proof to the phase-2 delivery mechanism (\"validate ONE mechanism, not two\"). But phase 1 is explicitly a *separate, earlier* step with a non-loading callback. Forcing phase 1 to adopt phase-2's constraints (a known-safe dispatch point, a measured Func offset, a confirmed-ticking BP target) imports phase-2 risk into a proof that could otherwise be done with a zero-engine-write context hijack (G1). **What closes it:** state the coupling as a deliberate trade-off, and consider proving GT-01 with the simplest mechanism first, then separately validating the Func-swap as the phase-2 carrier.\n\n### G3 — The recommended mechanism runs phase-2's blocking load at a NESTED, mid-script-VM re-entrancy point, and this hazard is not in the failure-mode list.\nThe trampoline fires *inside* `UFunction::Invoke` *inside* `ProcessEvent` *inside* the BP tick's own `ProcessEvent`. Phase-2's `LoadAsset_Blocking` → `FlushAsyncLoading` therefore runs re-entrantly nested in the script VM. In Shipping the reentrancy `check()`s are compiled out (`DO_CHECK==0`), so a bad nesting corrupts **silently**. This is precisely the safety advantage the *rejected* Ticker family had — it drains at a clean point between systems, not mid-VM. The design trades the Ticker's ABI hazard for a reentrancy hazard and discusses only the ABI side. **What closes it:** add nested-reentrancy to `failure_modes`; argue from source (or concede) that `FlushAsyncLoading` nested inside a tick `ProcessEvent` is safe in this build; note the visible multi-ms GameThread hitch.\n\n### G4 — The proof oracle's \"two INDEPENDENT methods\" is overstated; method_2(a) reuses method_1's value.\nMethod_2(a) is `GetCurrentThreadId() == TID_E1` — a direct comparison against method_1's output, not an independent derivation. The RSP-range check also derives from method_1's TEB fingerprint (E3). The *only* genuinely independent internal signal is method_2(b): `retAddr0 → UFunction::Invoke` (engine-dispatched vs. our-own-thread). **What closes it:** reframe honestly as \"one externally-established identity (doubly-derived via E1==E2) + one independent *provenance* fact (invoked by engine dispatch).\" These prove two *different propositions* (\"which OS thread\" and \"invoked by engine\"), which is genuinely valuable — but they are not two independent measurements of the same fact, and \"which thread is the GameThread\" has exactly one source of truth (the external naming). Do not sell it as more.\n\n### G5 — Phase 2 silently expands scope beyond the ONE call the owner authorized.\nThe owner authorized \"ONE call of `LoadAsset_Blocking`.\" The design adds a **second** GameThread-executed engine call, `MakeSoftObjectPath`, as a prerequisite (to intern the FNames). That is a real, defensible engineering need — but it is an unauthorized additional callback and must go back to the owner as an explicit re-scope, not be folded in. **What closes it:** surface it; get sign-off for exactly two named reflection calls, or find a single-call path.\n\n### G6 — The Func-offset population test's primary leg leans on an unverified static table — the ESC-02 defect pattern.\nLeg 1 (matching live `Func` values to statically-predicted native exec-thunk RVAs like `0x37445f0`) is only as good as the UHT `FNameNativePtrPair` extraction, which is *asserted* (\"Anchor Report 1\"), not shown here. If that parse is subtly wrong the \"unanimity\" can still land on a wrong offset if errors correlate. **Leg 2** — thousands of BP UFunctions sharing one identical `Func` value that also sits on a `.pdata` function start — is independent of the static table and is the trustworthy leg. **What closes it:** make leg 2 the *primary* acceptance criterion, demote leg 1 to corroboration, and BLOCK if the two legs disagree. Do not accept the offset on leg-1 unanimity alone.\n\n### G7 — The EventGraphFunction gate rests on a FALSE rationale, but source shows the gate is probably trivially satisfied in the cooked build — for a reason the design never states.\nThe design says \"ReceiveTick is immune anyway, it has a float parm.\" That is **wrong**: the fast-call validates `ParmsSize == sizeof(EventGraphParams)` where `EventGraphParams` is an `int32` = 4 bytes, and a float `DeltaSeconds` is also 4 bytes — a float parm does not make it immune. The real protection is `EventGraphFunction==null`. And here is the substantive finding: the **only** runtime writer of `->EventGraphFunction` is BlueprintGeneratedClass.cpp:475, inside `#if UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` **and** inside `WITH_EDITORONLY_DATA` (patched from `FastCallPairs_DEPRECATED`, comment: \"can't bump engine version to serialize it directly\"). `UE_BLUEPRINT_EVENTGRAPH_FASTCALLS` defaults to 1 (Build.h:365), but the *populating* code is editor-only. Strong implication: **in the cooked Shipping target, `EventGraphFunction` is null for all BP functions, the fast-call branch at ScriptCore.cpp:2044 is never taken, and ReceiveTick Func-swap is viable.** This *helps* the design — but it means the design hedged toward a widget out of a concern that likely doesn't apply, on a rationale that is factually wrong. **What closes it:** drop the float-parm claim; confirm live that the target's `EventGraphFunction==null` (expected, per source); and note this inverts the widget-vs-actor preference.\n\n### G8 — Internal contradiction: \"nothing persistent left behind\" is false as written.\n`why_narrowest` says the mechanism \"self-restores with nothing persistent left behind,\" while `restore_and_rollback` and the residuals commit to a **permanent resident DLL** (never `FreeLibrary`) and a **permanent `VirtualAllocEx` page**. The *hook* is not persistent; those two artifacts are (until process exit). **What closes it:** fix the wording to \"no persistent hook / no persistent engine-memory mutation,\" and confirm the owner's \"no persistent hook\" constraint tolerates a permanent resident module per run.\n\n### G9 — Operational gap: how does the read-only instrument find the record page?\nThe injector `VirtualAllocEx`s a page at a randomized address; the read-only ERI instrument must read it to render the verdict, but the address hand-off is unspecified. **What closes it:** specify an out-of-band manifest (injector writes the page base + layout to a run-manifest file the instrument reads).\n\n### G10 — E2's entry-point assumption is a single point of silent failure not gated as one.\nE2 requires the initial thread's `Win32StartAddress == base + AddressOfEntryPoint`. If anti-cheat/DRM starts the process **suspended** or shims the entry (residual acknowledges the possibility), E2 breaks and the E1==E2 cross-check silently degrades to E1 alone — losing half the external-identity proof without flagging it. **What closes it:** make E2's derivation a hard pre-flight gate that, on failure, explicitly downgrades the confidence claim rather than quietly proceeding on E1.\n\n---\n\n## On the questions posed directly\n\n- **Is the negative control real?** Yes, but weak. N1 can only fail if the recorder is broken; a correct recorder passes trivially. The experiment as a whole *can* genuinely fail (hitCount==0 timeout, tid≠TID_E1, retAddr not in .text are all real non-pass branches), so it is **not** constructed to always pass. But N1 itself is a sanity check, not a strong control — do not overstate its evidential weight.\n- **Is any address a guess dressed as derivation?** The Func offset is honestly flagged as unmeasured. The residual risk is G6 (leg-1 static table). The verified-address discipline is otherwise good: target UFunction by reflection, ProcessEvent by proven slot 77, entry-point from the PE header, `.text` range from section headers via live base.\n- **Does it honor every owner constraint verbatim?** Mostly, with two frictions: G5 (phase-2 adds an unauthorized second call) and G8 (the \"nothing persistent\" overstatement vs. the deliberate permanent leak).\n\nNet: the mechanism's core is source-accurate and the exactly-once/restore-first logic is sound. The design's real weaknesses are (1) it never surveyed the zero-engine-write families that may be narrower than its own recommendation (G1), (2) it couples a low-risk phase-1 proof to a higher-risk phase-2 carrier by choice (G2/G3), and (3) it carries one wrong supporting rationale (G7 float-parm) and one unverified static-table dependency (G6) into otherwise careful gating."}]}
